## 2022-08-09 · reply to @Simeon_Cps

> @Simeon_Cps I'm not sure if impacts short timelines much - the fabs will take years to build, and fabs in the US are a lot more controllable than fabs in Taiwan, so it might increase governance levers. Hard to say though

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1557128817050677248 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220810064710/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1557128817050677248)

## 2022-08-09 · reply to @Simeon_Cps

> @Simeon_Cps Chips isn't gonna really change supply of frontier GPUs massively. It's a lot more oriented around cpus and memory.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1557128118363533312 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220810013718/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1557128118363533312)

## 2022-08-09 · reply to @Simeon_Cps

> @Simeon_Cps Much of CHIPs is about reshoring parts of semiconductor supply chain so US isn't as dependent on other places for chips, including specialist US military chils. This makes policymakers more relaxed and reduces likelihood of extreme actions being taken in the future re supply.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1557127878734594050 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220809221435/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1557127878734594050)

## 2022-08-09 · reply to @Simeon_Cps

> @Simeon_Cps Chips act mostly pushes on diff capabilities to those needed for frontier AI dev, so probably reduces risk by increasing robustness of gov.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1557125965284708352 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220810032315/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1557125965284708352)

## 2022-08-08 · reply to @utopiannotions

> @utopiannotions Eventually, yes. I think people are underestimating risk in current era from governments feeling like they have an 'AI gap' and wildly overreacting, catalyzing extreme racing.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556432613300088832 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808001144/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556432613300088832)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @dpaleka

> @dpaleka Sky is gonna be blue when we build AGI, as well.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556408437562478592 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220808011111/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556408437562478592)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> @mattparlmer @VenemousTurnip Personally, I think the fact these ideas ignore geopolitical realities and are tin-eared about human culture means they're non-starters, but it doesn't stop other people advocating for these ideas and thinking they're good.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556393875362553859 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807213731/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556393875362553859)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @mattparlmer

> @mattparlmer @VenemousTurnip Many of the proposed ideas for resolving this stuff depend on panopticon, eg this Bostrom idea https://t.co/oIfzD3ClK1. I was trying to cheerfully indicate the impracticality of this with the end of my tweet.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556393629198848001 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807215233/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556393629198848001)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @VenemousTurnip

> @VenemousTurnip @mattparlmer yup!

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556388670529146881 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807211740/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556388670529146881)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> Trivia: the original developer of YOLO ceased developing it after V3 [2018: https://t.co/VqAb0Szps0] due to reasons outlined below -  - yolo (now v7) has subsequently been pushed forward by Taiwan/Russia/China. Some things are kind of locked-in... [Amazing paper overall btw!] https://t.co/11zol1ncpS

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556199730312425477 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807142740/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556199730312425477)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> Surveillance has already been radically changed by AI. Even public open source models (e.g YOLO) are perfectly usable, downloadable, and free. This means  you can track people. But that's not the interesting part...

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556196480465002496 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807083438/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556196480465002496)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @ilkekaya

> @ilkekaya @ylecun hey if you're gonna do an ad hominem take it off thread plz!

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556194592617222144 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807110947/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556194592617222144)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> Deepfakes have mostly been a porn thing, but we've had a few cases in politics (eg Gabon a few years ago, and some stuff in Ukraine). Deepfakes on smartphones is gonna be a thing soon - models appear and get miniaturized and open-sourced then made easy to use. Proliferation++

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556189771055054849 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807094230/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556189771055054849)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> Malware is bad now but will be extremely bad in the future due to intersection of RL + code models + ransomware economic incentives. That train is probably 1-2 years away based on lag of open source replication of existing private models, but it's on the tracks.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556181432522797056 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807073322/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556181432522797056)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> @ylecun There are also risks here - eg anti-nuke memes set back energy prices, and gray goo meme may have stunted nanotech dev. Not trying to spark that - AI is I think one of the silver bullets needed to radically improve all human life!

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556175533385256963 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807071035/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556175533385256963)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> @ylecun My general feeling is AI is like climate change - we're making systems that are altering the (mostly digital) world. But we have way less shared instrumentation for AI risk vs climate change risk, so I'm advocating for 'better safe than sorry.'

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556175037278740480 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807170741/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556175037278740480)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> 'street-level AI ' has already begun to change the nature of military conflict. It started in ~2015-ish, but in Ukraine this year we saw troops pair $10-20k drones with 1950s grenades and 3D-printed aero-fins, targeted via vision models trained to spot soldiers in camo.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556170100331319296 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807071712/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556170100331319296)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @CharlesRollet1

> @CharlesRollet1 By well-developed I mean 'have policies they can enforce'. In the US, we don't have many (any?) Ai policies and our enforcement capacity is v limited. Obviously China has diff values to us, but my point is china has better ability to enforce policy choices than us.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556168078240522240 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807064056/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556168078240522240)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> A lot of the mismatch between AI impact and societal benefit is due to a lack of ad-hoc technical experimentation capacity on part of govs/civil society wrt frontier models. They may have access to models, but you need to invest people time to help them explore. Hire a Virgil.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556163147399958528 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807084201/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556163147399958528)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> Building third-party AI auditing organizations via gov is challenging due to needing to wire-in funding. However, doing this in private sector requires CEO with vision  most people who could do this are either not willing to go FT on it, or feel time is better spent at AI cos.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556146791271174145 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807055749/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556146791271174145)

## 2022-08-07 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> For years, people built models then built safety tooling around them. People are now directly injecting safety into models via reinforcement learning for human feedback. Everyone is DIY'ing these values, so the values are subjective via the tastes of people within each org.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556133757333123072 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807071231/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556133757333123072)

## 2022-08-06 · reply to @nptacek

> @nptacek Yes, I think you need to bake safety into these models via leveraging human feedback, rather than doing plumbing on top.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556052494815350785 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220806230059/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556052494815350785)

## 2022-08-06 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> Code models are going to change some of the game theory of cyber offense/defense dynamics and the capabilities are going to cause real problems.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556051666239033344 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220806230202/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556051666239033344)

## 2022-08-06 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> Governments tend to literally not know how much money they spend on AI. Getting accurate funding data is extremely difficult, even for civil/public areas (been working on this for years via @indexingai ). You can't manage what you can't measure.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556051408318709761 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807025134/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556051408318709761)

## 2022-08-06 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> @mobav0 Finally, anything which influences the existential existence of a stage tends to get woven into the state (e.g, way we regulate utilities and energy), so I'm saying it's probably better to get ahead of this and actively do public-private things, before govts do it themselves.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556045456190291969 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220806223306/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556045456190291969)

## 2022-08-06 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> @mobav0 There's also the question of values - democracy is notionally built to let citizens get preferences represented via policy. It's not clear what rights, if any, people will have with regard to private sector developed AI, nor how they get values injected.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556044937149313024 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220806223010/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556044937149313024)

## 2022-08-06 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> AI is so strategic to so many companies that it has altered the dynamics of semiconductor development. Because chips take years to develop, we should expect drastic improvements in AI efficiency in the future, which has big implications on diffusion of capabilities.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556037290631188480 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220806220039/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556037290631188480)

## 2022-08-06 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> There are endless debates in policy forums about how important it is to focus on symbolic systems as well as deep learning systems. It's frequently unclear what people mean by symbolic and there aren't many benchmarks (any?) where symbolic systems show up. Dark matter.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556036716065988608 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807030831/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556036716065988608)

## 2022-08-06 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> Policy is a gigantic random number generator - some random event might trigger some politician to have a deep opinion about an aspect of AI, after which they don't update further. This can brick long-term projects randomly (very relaxing).

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556023320344027136 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220807030709/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556023320344027136)

## 2022-08-06 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> To get stuff done in policy you have to be wildly specific. CERN for AI? Cute idea. Now tell me about precise funding mechanisms, agency ownership, plan for funding over long-term. If you don't do the details, you don't get stuff done.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556021224823595008 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220806213632/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556021224823595008)

## 2022-08-06 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> Most policy forums involve people giving canned statements of their positions, and everyone thanks eachother for giving their positions, then you agree it was good to have a diverse set of perspectives, then the event ends. Huge waste of everyone's time.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556020940613398528 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220806205527/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1556020940613398528)

## 2022-07-11 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> The idea here is to catalyze activity in studying, auditing, and assessing the traits of widely-used and/or deployed AI systems - from YOLO to CLIP, from GPT3 to GPT-NeoX, and so on. For AI to benefit society, we need society to have more critical faculties wrt AI.

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1546552703978860544 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220711175228/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1546552703978860544)

## 2022-07-11

> Do you want to audit a deployed or open source model for societal impact? Do you want to use, or create, open source software tools to do this? Would you like a chance at a $25,000 first prize? If so, please enter the AI Audit Challenge! https://t.co/qmLBxootfu

Quoting https://x.com/StanfordHAI/status/1546524735340548096:
> Introducing the #AIAuditChallenge – a $71K competition to design better AI audits. @StanfordHAI &amp; @StanfordCyber are seeking proposals for tools that enable regulators, journalists &amp; civil society to effectively audit deployed AI systems/open source models.https://t.co/oDIJTgRHdX https://t.co/wvL9MAZryN

https://x.com/jackclarkSF/status/1546552272389214208 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220711175040/https://twitter.com/jackclarkSF/status/1546552272389214208)

## 2022-07-01 · reply to @jackclarkSF

> Both of these results were published TODAY. These results happen at a delay, so this is probably old information on order of 3-9 months. There are easily 5 labs and probably 10 with enough compute to play at this level. Imagine what we don't know right now?

> Video recording of my @indexingai talk is now available - fast-forward to 36 mins in for the Q&amp;A - we got fantastic questions from the audience and got into some spicier stuff re country competition, AI ethics, AI safety, increasingly general systems, etc https://t.co/ZbGVjUn1Zu
